campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:7: > - The Dock Ward is Waterdeep's port ward, a district of piers, shipyards, warehouses and tenements that holds, with the Field Ward, most of the city's poor. Xanathar's Guild treats it as its main surface territory, and Manshoon's Zhentarim fight a low-level street war against it here.

campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:191: - The streets are steep and hard to read. Shop signs appear only near the piers, and warehouses often have none, so a visitor either knows where to go or is lost and a likely mark for pickpockets. Streetlamps lose their candles and glass as fast as the lamplighters replace them, and locals carry their own light at night. Yellow chalk eye marks on alley walls show Xanathar's territory to anyone who knows them.

Harpers provide cover for one sensitive operation per quest: forged documents, coordinated distractions, or witnesses who saw nothing. Harper informants embedded in Xanathar's Guild, Sea Maidens Faire, and the Splinter can be activated for one specific intelligence request each per quest (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). A second *persona* is learned. When any villain faction designates a character as a priority target, Mirt's network detects it and passes warning within 24 hours. |

campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:7: The Order keeps a fixed order of threats: diabolism first, aberrant evil second, everything else after that. The vault gold concerns Savra only as a function of who might seize it. Her fear is that 500,000 gold would fund the thing she suspects the Sea Ward family of doing, and she cannot yet say what that is. In the Splinter's or Xanathar's hands, it would be nearly as bad. In the city treasury, she has no objection.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:31: | **Xanathar's Lair** | The Guild's use of intellect devourers is an abomination in Savra's theological framework. She asks the party to put Nihiloor down for good. Nihiloor always escapes, so she asks for the attempt and not the kill. A Whitehawk member also gets her lair support: she waits across from the guildsign entrance and fights beside the party if called, which spends the quest's one Marcheon operation. |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:51: - **Rescue a mind-controlled or enslaved Waterdhavian (+1).** This covers freeing a citizen from an intellect devourer, breaking a charm placed by a Xanathar operative, or liberating someone held against their will. It pays once for each significant rescue.

campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:47: - **Purge an intellect devourer infestation or free a mind-controlled Waterdhavian (+1).** Directly countering Xanathar's mind-control operations is Force Grey's most urgent Grand Game priority. A single implanted citizen counts as one incident; discovering and purging an entire network counts as two.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:73: | **M3 Complete** plus **Xanathar's Lair** preparation, or Renown 8 | **Destroy the Intellect Factory** | An objective inside **Xanathar's Lair**, played in Nihiloor's wing. Destroy the pool that breeds Occupying Devourers, take the placement records and free the captive. Nihiloor always escapes. Vajra pays for any *raise dead* the work needs. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:9: Rather than competing with Xanathar or Manshoon for the Stone, Jarlaxle already holds Eye #3, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* and mounted beneath the *Eyecatcher*'s keel, and is content to let the other factions exhaust themselves chasing each other. His asset inside Xanathar's organization — **Nar'l Xibrindas**, a drow mage who serves as the beholder's advisor and reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — keeps him informed of the Guild's movements. The Sea Maidens Faire parades give him cover to move personnel and materials through any ward without scrutiny.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:15: - At Renown 3+: Nar'l Xibrindas's intelligence on Xanathar's movement patterns is available once per tenday — response timing and surface contacts, not the lair layout. The distinction is deliberate.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:47: - **Protect Nar'l Xibrindas's cover within Xanathar's Guild (+1).** Any action that deflects suspicion from Nar'l, discredits a threat to his position, or removes someone who has come close to identifying him earns D'aerthe renown — whether or not Jarlaxle explicitly requested it.

Access to the *Sea Maidens Faire* for cover identity support and discreet meetings. Bregan D'aerthe fences: 20% discount on black-market purchases brokered through the company. |

Three approaches: feed him a false report, extract him from the city, or confront Ahmaergo's counterintelligence source. | +3 |

campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:136: - *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* (Xanathar's Lair)

campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:1: # Xanathar's Guild
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:3: Game mechanics for running Xanathar's Guild as a villain faction. For lore, operations, and key members, see the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:7: Xanathar deploys response teams in escalating tiers. The first or second team the party encounters carries *A Directive from Nar'l Xibrindas*, instructing the team leader to resupply at Grinda Garloth's. If it is the second encounter, the directive notes the party's previous success and names the fallen agents. That team also carries a *potion of healing*, a *potion of greater healing*, and a *+1 weapon*.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:13: **Third Team — Noska's Team.** Noska Ur'gray leads six **bugbears** and a **gazer**. He is sent when Xanathar is done sending messages and wants the problem gone.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:21: A modest rowhouse secretly owned by Xanathar, the Terasse Estate operates on two levels — both under the same roof, both accessible by passphrase, and neither officially connected. Keln Trigos runs a healing operation for Guild agents on the upper floors. Below it, the estate serves as one of Waterdeep's gladiatorial gathering points: those who want to attend the fights in Xanathar's Lair report here; staff ply them with exotic food, then blindfold them and escort them through an underground dock to caverns near Skullport.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:35: ***Escalation.*** Xanathar moves to Suspicious (Tier 2). If a second Xanathar outpost has already been hit, it moves directly to Alert (Tier 3), and Gorath's team deploys within 2 days to the last known party location. **Founders' Day Clock: 2–4 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:39: *Independent discovery path: any character asking about Xanathar weapons, black-market arms, or Guild suppliers in the Dock Ward is pointed to Grinda in Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma check, no faction membership required). This is the Xanathar chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:41: Grinda runs her operation out of the standard Mistshore description, ignoring seasonal variants. She is Xanathar's black market armorer, equipping Guild agents on request and freelancing to third parties for coin.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:43: **Area D2 — Grinda's Chest.** 500 gp and the *Note Regarding Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Ahmaergo, confirming the weapon delivery to Terasse Estate and citing Xanathar's expectation of "exotic gutting."
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:49: ***Escalation.*** Xanathar moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the location is raided; no tier change if the party engages as customers without taking documents. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:51: ### Orb Confectioners / Xanathar Sewer Hideout — Encounter-Plus (Southern Ward, Rising Ride)
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:59: **Area B9b — Korgstrod's Room.** The chest at the foot of the bed is a **mimic**. A hidden coffer under the bed (DC 13 to find) holds 500 gp and *Correspondence with Nar'l Xibrindas*: "Only you, me, and X know of the smokepowder. It must be brought to the Staircase of Eyes on the night of the 1st at precisely the stroke of eleven. Use disposable muscle. I will mark the sewer tunnels west of your hideout with a red eye to guide your men." Nar'l's guildsigns lead toward Area X1 of Xanathar's Lair; the trail requires DC 15 checks, rising to DC 17 near the lair itself. Three failures before a success means the trail is lost.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:63: ***Escalation.*** Xanathar moves to Alert (Tier 3). Korgstrod's response team deploys within 3 days of the operation. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–3 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:67: Xanathar's paranoia means its response to ambiguity is escalation. A single threat to an outpost pushes the faction directly from Suspicious to Alert, skipping the intermediate tier.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:74: | Lockdown | Third team (Noska's bugbear squad with orders to eliminate). Xanathar takes personal interest; lair incursions trigger Eye-ray responses |
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:77: > Anything involving Sylgar — Xanathar's goldfish — brings the faction directly to Lockdown without warning, regardless of the current tier. No prior incident is required. No warning shot follows.

campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: *Backroom.* A rack and other instruments of torture. An arcane circle on the floor. Strapped into the rack is **Ott Steeltoes**, a Xanathar's Guild agent captured while the Zhentarim were trying to locate the Eye. Ott put the Eye in Sylgar's bowl as a pretty stone and has no idea what it is.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:27: If the party frees him, Ott immediately tries to recruit his rescuers into Xanathar's organization. With patience and the right questions, the party can persuade him to lead them toward the Lair instead.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:31: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3). Flying snakes carry the alarm within hours if any escape; Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 3 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**

campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:11: If the party refused or turned Hostile, the campaign takes a harder shape. Imps summoned at the Asmodean Shrine maintain surveillance on the characters, feeding the Cassalanters continuous intelligence. Seffia's team joins the Eye heist at **Xanathar's Lair** as a competing faction. Willifort attempts to assassinate Jarlaxle, fails, then targets the characters in a follow-up attempt. This may push Jarlaxle to propose an alliance against the Cassalanters.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:9: **All four villain factions run simultaneously.** No season villain. Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Cassalanters, and Bregan D'aerthe are active from session one, each pursuing a different angle on the same prize.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:11: **Act III is four distinct heist genres.** Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration; Cassalanter Villa is social penetration; Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper; Kolat Towers is a fortress assault. Each target is scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:32: **Feature All Villains** means keeping all four factions moving between sessions. Faction Response Teams deploy when the party hits assets; the party's choices shift which factions gain ground. Each faction responds to threat differently: Xanathar escalates on instinct, Manshoon prefers capture over kill, the Cassalanters exhaust legal and social leverage before calling up infernal muscle. That difference should be legible at the table. See [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) for the between-session tracking procedure.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:48: The MacGuffin chain runs in a straight line: the Stone of Golorr names the vault but can't open it without all three Eyes. Xanathar holds Eye #1 (in Sylgar's fish bowl, unknowingly). The Cassalanters hold Eye #2. Jarlaxle holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Kolat Towers holds Manshoon, not an Eye. It's the fourth heist target; clearing it before the Vault pushes the party to Level 7.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:11: | III | The Heists | 4–7 | Faction Outposts, Xanathar's Lair, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Maidens Faire, Kolat Towers | 16–28 | Can the PCs recover all three Eyes before any faction assembles them? |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:29: Five quests, four heist targets, no fixed order. The party holds the Stone and needs three Eyes to open the Vault. Faction Outposts provides the intelligence to plan. Three of the four lair heists hold an Eye; Kolat Towers holds Manshoon. Each target is built as a genuine operation: scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares. Xanathar's Lair is a dungeon infiltration into the criminal undercity. Cassalanter Villa is social penetration into a noble household with a cult temple beneath it. The Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper aboard three ships. Kolat Towers is a fortress assault against Manshoon's extradimensional sanctum. Two clocks run underneath all of it: the Faire departs Tarsakh 20 and Founders' Day falls Flamerule 1. Act III is the bulk of the campaign. Thorough planning pays off; the structure supports improvisation.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:56: > | 7 | The lair heists (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**): none of them can be done alone. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:104: The job was supposed to be clean. Xanathar's Guild was acquiring something from a rival organization, and you were hired to facilitate transit and paid well enough not to ask questions. What came out of there wasn't what you'd been told. What you left behind was considerably worse. The Guild got its item. The irregularities were absorbed into a larger accounting of that night, and your involvement went officially unnoted.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:106: Ahmaergo, Xanathar's dwarven majordomo and the man who actually runs the Guild's logistics, keeps a private ledger separate from the official records. Your name is in it, under a column that reads *Outstanding*.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:110: ### The Holder: Ahmaergo (Xanathar's Guild)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:112: Ahmaergo is the majordomo of Xanathar's Guild: a shield dwarf of outward civility and exacting administrative competence who functions as the Guild's operational second-in-command. Most orders that bear Xanathar's name were drafted by Ahmaergo. His private ledger is separate from official Guild records because Xanathar doesn't know about every arrangement he makes, which is precisely the point.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:117: > Ahmaergo keeps his private ledger separate from Guild records because Xanathar doesn't know about every arrangement he runs. If Xanathar becomes aware of the character's existence — through carelessness, another character's indiscretion, or any direct contact with the Guild — the dynamic changes entirely. Xanathar doesn't distinguish between asset, debtor, and enemy when its paranoia is engaged. Hold the beholder back from this relationship for as long as dramatically possible.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:155: Skeemo Weirdbottle maintains, out of a cramped shop on a side street in the Trades Ward, one of the most comprehensive indices of Waterdeep's criminal and semi-criminal activities that exist outside Xanathar's Guild's own records. He compiled it partly for business and partly because he finds the city's power structures intrinsically interesting. He sold you something from it (access, a specific piece of information, a document that enabled something you needed to do) and conducted the transaction with professional discretion.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:15: The remix runs all four simultaneously for three reasons. First, a city where four powerful factions are genuinely competing gives players organic reasons to act without the plot needing to push them. If the Cassalanters are poisoning people, Xanathar is having informants killed, and Manshoon is blackmailing city officials, the party doesn't need a quest to care. Second, four-faction play creates genuine choice: which lair do you hit first? Whose Eye do you trade for an alliance? A single-villain campaign has only one answer to those questions. Third, no single villain defines the campaign's identity, which means it can accommodate different playstyles at once. The group that wants political intrigue gets it through the Cassalanters; the group that wants dungeon delving gets it through Xanathar's Lair.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:27: The four lairs run as four distinct genres. Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration: break into an underground complex with a beholder at the center. Cassalanter Villa is social infiltration: work within a functioning household whose owners are watching you back. Sea Maidens Faire is a caper: the thing that's supposed to go wrong goes wrong in the first act, and you adapt in real time. Kolat Towers is a raid: Manshoon isn't a puzzle, he's an obstacle, and the question is whether you can take him. Different player instincts dominate different genres, so the heist phase plays differently from session to session rather than feeling like four iterations of the same map.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:43: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the party spends three sessions pursuing Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon haven't been idle. Keep a one-line answer ready for what each faction did during that time. When PCs canvass contacts, read Watch reports, or press informants, they may turn up traces of that off-screen activity. The world feels inhabited rather than scripted when the factions are visibly busy with their own agendas.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:49: **Xanathar** escalates faster than any other faction and less rationally. Its paranoia means a single unexplained loss can push it from Suspicious to Alert without any intermediate step. Anything touching Sylgar, its goldfish, bypasses the tier sequence and produces Lockdown behavior immediately. When Xanathar reaches Alert or Lockdown, plan for overreaction: more force than the situation warrants, a readiness to burn assets to make a point.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:61: Manshoon's Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild are in active conflict at street level in the Dock Ward. Manshoon's operatives probe Guild territory regularly; Xanathar has issued a kill order on Manshoon that has failed twice. This is a grinding low-level engagement that produces casualties on both sides and keeps both factions consuming resources on a front entirely separate from the PCs.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:63: Jarlaxle's relationship with Xanathar is more managed. Nar'l Xibrindas, Xanathar's own senior advisor, reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell. The intelligence Nar'l provides has been selectively curated to keep the beholder occupied and predictable; Jarlaxle's goal is a manageable Xanathar, not a dead one. Xanathar suspects a mole in its organization and has been hunting in the wrong direction. It has not identified Nar'l.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:72: > This is the Grand Game's self-reinforcing pressure. If the PCs dismantle Xanathar's Guild outposts, Manshoon's Zhentarim moves into the vacated Dock Ward territory. If the PCs weaken Manshoon, Xanathar's paranoia briefly lifts and it turns outward. The PCs' choices produce real chain reactions because thinking factions respond to changing conditions. The inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains** shows where each faction's active operations overlap and which documents cross faction lines; consult it whenever the party clears territory a rival faction would want.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:90: **Xanathar** rarely backs down once engaged. It takes opposition personally, its grudges are permanent, and it has no diplomatic instincts. Standing down is possible only if the PCs perform an act that satisfies its ego: a gesture of submission, a gift of intelligence about one of its enemies, a public humiliation of someone it hates. The act must be specific and visible to it. Quiet de-escalation doesn't register.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:99: - [Setting: Villains](../../setting/villains/) — personality phases and goals for Xanathar, Manshoon, the Cassalanters, and Jarlaxle
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:101: - [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md) — response teams, revelation list
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:163: | Doom Raiders | **Davil Starsong** (Missions 1–2); **Tashlyn Yafeera** (Missions 3–6) | Intelligence on Manshoon's cell; containment of Xanathar's Guild activity; anything that weakens their rivals |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:200: The seven factions above are the organizations available for player characters to join. The other major powers active in Waterdeep during the Grand Game — **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter**, and the **Cassalanters** — are adversary factions. Players may interact with, negotiate with, or broker deals with these organizations, but formal membership is not available through normal play. See **Notable Figures** for details on their leaders and agents, and **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for how to run them at the table.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:19: **The Stone of Golorr** — an aboleth creation. Xanathar holds it in its lair until Dalakhar, a rock gnome spy in Neverember's employ, steals it on Ches 20; he was carrying it to Trollskull Manor when the fireball killed him on Ches 22. A Gralhund nimblewright took it from Dalakhar's body and fled across the rooftops. The party acquires it during **Gralhund Villa**. The Stone can name the location of Neverember's Vault, but Neverember blinded it by removing three Eyes before losing control of it. Without all three Eyes seated in the Stone, it reveals nothing useful.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:21: **Eye #1** — formerly held by Manshoon's Zhentarim, who stole it from Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. Xanathar killed the Zhentarim envoys who brought it to him and seized the Eye. It now resides in Xanathar's Lair sanctum.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** Event Outcome set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:28: > **Eye #1:** Xanathar — in Sylgar's bowl, Xanathar's Lair. Xanathar does not know what it is.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:30: > **Eye #3:** Jarlaxle Baenre — aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is. It passed from Renaer's locket to the Zhentarim, then to Xanathar's Guild in the warehouse raid, and Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. Manshoon holds no Eye.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/03-staff-and-hiring.md:66: | 3 | **Whisper** | N kenku | Does not speak. Writes her name on a card, then writes: "I can reproduce any dish I have tasted or observed. Cannot improvise; if a supply is unavailable, waits for it rather than substituting. campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/03-staff-and-hiring.md:74: | 1 | **Yagra Stonefist** | N half-orc | Party met her in Act I. campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q07-boss-chamber.md:39: ### Xanathar Sewer Hideout Event
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q07-boss-chamber.md:41: During the **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** Event, Grum'shar and Nihiloor are present when the party arrives (unless the failure state applies). campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:5: In the center of the floor stands a stone pillar, roughly five feet tall and two feet in diameter, carved from a single piece of dark granite. The carving on its face is precise and deliberate: a circle of ten equidistant spokes (Xanathar's guildsign) centered on a small circular indentation at chest height. It is approximately three inches in diameter, shaped like a lidless eye, slightly recessed, smooth at the center and beveled at the edges — built to receive something specific.
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:11: **Activation:** Nihiloor's 3-inch stone orb, when pressed into the indentation, opens an opaque black doorway 8 feet tall and 4 feet wide in the south wall. The portal leads to area X22 of Xanathar's lair. Any creature that steps through appears in X22. It remains open for 1 minute after the orb is removed, then closes. It can't be held open by other means.
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:21: **DC 16 Intelligence (History):** The guildsign on the pillar is Xanathar's personal mark, the lidless eye symbol used by Xanathar's Guild. This specific arrangement (circle, ten spokes, indentation at center) appears in accounts of Xanathar's lair architecture. The pillar was placed here deliberately, as a fixed transit point.
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:23: ### Xanathar's Lair Seed
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:25: This room is the campaign's first physical connection to Xanathar's lair. When the party infiltrates the lair in Xanathar's Lair, they will enter from the other side of this portal, arriving in X22, recognizing the transit architecture, and understanding that the pillar they examined in the sewers under Trollskull Alley is the same system they are now using. The party can't follow Nihiloor through in Finding Floon. By Xanathar's Lair, they will have the means.
campaign/locations/xanathar-sewer-hideout/q11-escape-portal.md:27: ### Xanathar Sewer Hideout Event

campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/area-overview.md:22: The warehouse sits at the back of a walled yard on Candle Lane. DC 10 Intelligence identifies it as the Zhentarim symbol.

The Zhentarim cell that used this warehouse is dead — killed in the raid that preceded the party's arrival.
campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/area-overview.md:52: The twelve corpses in Z01 are the remnants of two organizations that met violently. Five Zhentarim thugs (flying snake tattoos on neck or forearm) and seven Xanathar's Guild thugs, one of whom has Xanathar's circle-and-spokes mark tattooed on their right palm. Rapiers and daggers lie nearby. The fight was no more than a day old.

campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/z05-offices.md:11: This is the locket Renaer described. Whoever opened it took the Eye of the Stone of Golorr. campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:9: ***The Two Zhentarims in Finding Floon.*** Dragon Heist presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Finding Floon to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist (a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people) is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar's Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Trollskull Alley and then raid Kolat Towers in Kolat Towers will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1: one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. Players feel the distinction before anyone explains it.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:17: ***Nihiloor as Foreshadowing.*** Q07 in the sewer hideout is the campaign's first contact with Nihiloor. Xanathar's Lair eventually identifies it as the true intelligence driving Xanathar's Guild, running three parallel projects, exercising psionic control over Xanathar, and infiltrating Waterdeep's civil institutions (see the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page). In Finding Floon, Nihiloor doesn't fight them. It's simply there — seated, watching Grum'shar work — and then it's gone, faster than a creature that large has any right to move, through a portal they can't follow. This restraint is intentional; the encounter registers the creature's existence and its agency over the Guild cell before any context is available to understand what it is. Nihiloor's full scope surfaces across Trollskull Alley through Xanathar's Lair. By the time they reach Undermountain Level 17 in the Dungeon of the Mad Mage, its presence in Finding Floon should read as a thread that began in the sewers under Trollskull Alley.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:9: > - A bar brawl erupts between Yagra Stonefist (Doom Raiders) and Krentz's Xanathar's Guild crew
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:32: Within fifteen minutes of the party's arrival, Yagra Stonefist crosses the room. She has been nursing a drink while watching a table of five Xanathar's Guild thugs, led by **Krentz**, a man with eye-shaped tattoos on his shaved scalp. Two nights ago, Krentz's crew killed a Doom Raiders operative in the Dock Ward. When one of Krentz's companions says something audible about snake men getting what they deserved, Yagra moves.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:49: **The Most Trollskull Alley-Specific Faction Content.** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication. Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Trollskull Alley arrive at Xanathar's Lair with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:55: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** ev-06 and ev-07 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa. But a party that pursues them gains: the death mark (characterization of Xanathar's Guild's sophistication), the Cassalanter-to-vault link through Kalain's account of Victoro's visit, the vault-opening method, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the campaign, gated behind exactly the investigative habits this quest tries to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:92: > **Dalakhar** (up to 5 questions): Stole the *Stone of Golorr* from a beholder called Xanathar, deep in the dungeons beneath the city. Worked for the rightful Open Lord — Neverember, not Silverhand. The Stone is the key to a hidden hoard of gold dragons sealed somewhere in the city. He heard of adventurers who rescued Renaer Neverember and decided their hands were safer. Renaer vouched for them.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:182: > **Path B — Speak with Dead, Dalakhar.** Available for five days. Five answers available; relevant questions yield: the Stone is the key to a hidden vault of gold dragons; he stole it from Xanathar's lair; Renaer vouched for them.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:40: 4. **The death mark.** On the pillow, placed deliberately: a **disk of black stone**, flat, polished, palm-sized. One face painted with a stylized eye — ringed iris, vertical slit pupil. Xanathar's personal sigil. *We know where you sleep. We could have killed you.* The agents were working from stale intelligence when they left it. Dalakhar never saw it; he was already gone. DC 13 Intelligence (History) or DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana) to identify the symbol as Xanathar's personal sigil.

campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:45: If the chase fails and the Stone reaches a faction safe house, the campaign continues from a weaker position. Jarlaxle will deal with the party. Xanathar will not. Manshoon's cell now knows the party is active and failed. These consequences reshape the social landscape of subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is **Faction Outposts**' problem, not this quest's.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:57: Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's North Ward operations become more careful. Jarlaxle leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown (who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn) define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.

campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-08-the-rooftop-chase.md:54: ### Xanathar's Gazer Intervention
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-08-the-rooftop-chase.md:56: Xanathar's gazers have been following anyone who left the villa with something valuable. They may intercede. The GM chooses one of two outcomes:
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-08-the-rooftop-chase.md:58: **Tactical assist:** A gazer swoops in and fires a paralytic ray at whoever is carrying the Stone, crippling them for one round and letting the party close distance. Xanathar would rather the party have the Stone than any other faction; it can always take the Stone from the party later.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-08-the-rooftop-chase.md:60: **Response team intercept:** If the GM wants a harder version of this scene, a Xanathar's Guild response team cuts off the rooftop exit route, producing a three-way confrontation for the Stone. Refer to the Response Teams section of the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for roster and tactics.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:108: **Lords' Alliance (Jalester).** His debrief request arrives within twenty-four hours. The Watch officers on the Saerdoun Street beat were transferred to a Southern Ward assignment the week before the raid. Jalester has traced the reassignment to a request from inside the Watch hierarchy; the source is unknown. He wants to know if the party saw any evidence of Watch coordination with Xanathar's Guild or the Gralhund household.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:110: **Emerald Enclave (Melannor).** Gralhund Villa sits over a minor ley line the Enclave monitors as part of Jeryth Phaulkon's aberrant-activity survey. Xanathar's Guild's presence in the neighborhood destabilized it over the past week in a way that correlates with Nihiloor's movements below the city. Melannor wants to know what the party observed. For the map.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:114: **Force Grey (Vajra).** A full operational debrief: which factions were present, where each one entered and exited, whether any Watch checkpoint in the surrounding blocks was compromised or unmanned during the raid, and any direct evidence of Nihiloor's involvement. Xanathar's Guild activity approaching a Watch checkpoint the Guild has never previously targeted is, in her judgment, a test. She wants to know if the test succeeded.

Jarlaxle's Bregan D'aerthe has a team across the street, waiting for the right moment to move.
campaign/setting/deities/00-the-deities.md:182: Each entry is a fit that the power's page derives, not published rules. The cleric column gives the page's best domain and the book it comes from. XPHB is the 2024 Player's Handbook, FRHoF is Heroes of Faerûn, RHW is Ravenloft: The Horrors Within, AU is Arcana Unleashed, XGE is Xanathar's Guide to Everything, TCE is Tasha's Cauldron of Everything, and PHB 2014 and DMG 2014 are the 2014 books. Oath names are shortened, so Devotion means Oath of Devotion. Fits are judged by theme, so an option stands where the power's portfolio and the option's theme match, whether or not a book lists it for that power. A word in brackets is the page's own rating where the page still calls a fit weak. A fit with no bracket is one the page does not hedge. Most pages also name an alternative for each fit, and this table leaves those out. Two oaths are sourced: Heroes of Faerûn (FRHoF) names Oath of Devotion for **Torm** and **Ilmater**. Rows are sorted by cleric domain, in the order of the next table, then by name. A cell reading "None" means the page found no fit.

campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:60: > - **A paladin PC:** Oathbreaker (derived; Dungeon Master's Guide 2014). The subclass is a paladin who breaks sacred oaths to serve an evil power, and Asmodeus is a lawful evil archdevil, so a fallen knight who takes his terms is a natural reading. Oath of Conquest (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the alternative for a paladin who serves his rule by force.
campaign/setting/deities/auril.md:72: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Conquest (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything). The wiki quotes her charge to her priests as covering the lands with ice and making all Faerûn fear her, and it calls her the General of Winter's War. That is conquest by fear, which is what the oath is built around. Oath of Vengeance (Player's Handbook, 2024) is the alternative, since her Chosen hunt the agents of her enemies.
campaign/setting/deities/bahamut.md:69: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Devotion (derived; Player's Handbook 2024), a good fit. The wiki names two orders of his knights, the dragon paladins of the Talons of Justice and the dragonborn of the Platinum Cadre, and it says good clerics and paladins make pilgrimages to his palace. The oath's theme of justice and order and its knight archetype match the Ptarian Code's honor and protection of the lesser races. Oath of Redemption (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the alternative, for a follower who leads with mercy, since the oath asks a paladin to use violence only as a last resort and he would sooner talk an opponent into surrender than fight.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:70: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Conquest (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything). It is sworn by paladins who seek glory in battle and the subjugation of their enemies, which matches a faith that tells the strong to rule the weak and draws conquerors and fighters. The Knights of the Black Gauntlet take recruits of any class. Oath of the Crown (derived; Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide) is the alternative for a knight who serves the order's hierarchy.
campaign/setting/deities/bhaal.md:99: > - **No campaign tie, searched:** A search of `campaign/` for Bhaal, Bhaalist, Bhaalspawn, "Lord of Murder", "Dead Three" and "murder cult" returns only deity pages that list him as a foe or a Dead Three member (`tyr.md:43`, `ilmater.md:50`, `jergal.md:46`). A search of `campaign/setting/` and `campaign/guides/` for "assassin" finds Ziraj the Hunter, Urstul Floxin and Willifort Crowelle and several faction-mission lines, and none says an assassin is sworn to Bhaal. Bregan D'aerthe and Xanathar's Guild have no tie to him in any file, so the page adds none and invents no Waterdeep cult. `sources/adventure-wdmm.json` has no Bhaal entry.
campaign/setting/deities/corellon-larethian.md:66: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of the Ancients (derived; Player's Handbook, 2024). The oath is as old as the first elves, and its paladins preserve life and cherish the light, which suits the creator of the elves and the Preserver of Life. Oath of Redemption (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the alternative for the mercy, love and pity that the wiki says he prized most (*Monster Mythology*).
campaign/setting/deities/eilistraee.md:67: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Redemption (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything). Its features include Emissary of Peace and Rebuke the Violent, and it asks a paladin to use violence only as a last resort. That matches the Silverhair Knights, who fight only when they must and use only nonlethal techniques. A paladin of the Darksong Knights, who hunt yochlols and Lolth's other demonic agents, could take Oath of the Watchers instead (derived; Tasha's Cauldron of Everything), which binds paladins to protect mortal realms from extraplanar creatures.
campaign/setting/deities/eilistraee.md:118: > - **Names with no page:** Qilué Veladorn, Vhaeraun, Kiaransalee, Selvetarm, Araushnee, Erevan Ilesere, Lurue, Haela Brightaxe, Callarduran Smoothhands, the Seldarine, the Dark Seldarine, the Silverhair Knights, the Darksong Knights, the Sword Dancers, the Promenade of the Dark Maiden, the Dancing Haven, Darkmaiden's Leap, Menzoberranzan, Cormanthor, Silverymoon, Callidae and Skullport are plain text, since none is on the roster or in Notable Figures. campaign/setting/deities/garl-glittergold.md:63: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Redemption (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything), a good fit. MTF has him favor trickery and illusion over combat and use his axe to escape danger, and the oath asks a paladin to use violence only as a last resort. Oath of Devotion (derived; Player's Handbook, 2024) is the alternative for the Golden Watchful, a small knightly order of gnome paladins who serve him (wiki), and his title of Watchful Protector points to guarding others. campaign/setting/deities/gond.md:66: > - **A cleric PC:** The domains are Forge and Knowledge (SCAG, 2014). Forge Domain (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the best fit, because it is the first of his two domains and his portfolio is craft and smithwork, and its Blessing of the Forge and Artisan's Blessing features suit a priest who works at a bench. Knowledge Domain (derived; Heroes of Faerûn) is the alternative for an inventor who is more scholar than smith. The Divine Order that fits is Thaumaturge (derived), because his clergy are inventors and tinkers rather than guards, and the cantrip *Mending* suits a priest who repairs things. Valetta is the model of a cleric of Gond in Waterdeep.

campaign/setting/deities/moradin.md:61: > - **A cleric PC:** The domains are Forge and Knowledge (SCAG, 2014). Forge Domain (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the fit, since he is the master of every craft the dwarves practice. Its features include Blessing of the Forge and Artisan's Blessing. Knowledge Domain (derived; FRHoF) is the alternative for a cleric modeled on the priests who judge craftwork and keep the volumes of technique. The Divine Order that fits is Protector (derived), for the fighting clerics of the Hammers of Moradin and the chain mail and silvered helms the wiki gives his clergy. A Knowledge cleric who keeps the volumes would take Thaumaturge instead (derived).
campaign/setting/deities/talos.md:78: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Vengeance (derived; Player's Handbook, 2024). The wiki says he holds grudges for centuries, and Vengeance is the oath built around retribution. Oath of Conquest (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the alternative for a paladin who follows the clergy's example of winning converts through fear.
campaign/setting/deities/tempus.md:73: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Glory (derived; Player's Handbook 2024). The House of Heroes honors champions of battle and sport, and his code asks for bravery in the field, so the fit is a paladin who seeks renown through deeds. Oath of Conquest (derived; Xanathar's Guide to Everything) is the alternative for a paladin who seeks glory in battle itself.

Oath of Vengeance (derived; Player's Handbook 2024) is the alternative, since it is built on a solemn commitment to punish, which matches her habit of never forgiving a slight (*Monster Mythology*, via the wiki).

campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:9: > - **Quests and events:** **Finding Floon** opens in the Yawning Portal, where a Bregan D'aerthe observer watches the well (**The Yawning Portal**). **Trollskull Alley** plants three Undermountain seeds in **The Grand Opening**. **Xanathar's Lair** runs a tunnel to Skullport, and **Vault of Dragons** ends in the Undermountain handoff. The faction events that reach into the dungeon are **The Grells in the Dock Ward**, **Autumnreaver** and **The Dreamer's Reach** (Emerald Enclave), **The Stone's Other Master** (Harpers), **Lioncrown** (Lords' Alliance), **Houseless Noble** (Bregan D'aerthe) and **Force Grey Commander** (Force Grey).
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:17: Skullport is a grim, cutthroat town in a cavern on the River Sargauth, connected to Undermountain's third level. Xanathar's Guild holds it, and the campaign places it outside the city's jurisdiction. Few people from the surface know the way there, and the people who live there prefer it like that.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:39: - **The Castle Ward sewers:** A spiral staircase behind a secret door in the sewers leads to Xanathar's lair. Dragon Heist says it bypasses a harder route through Undermountain and Skullport, which is why most parties never walk that route.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:67: | 2 | Arcane Chambers | Xanathar's Guild keeps two watch posts. Rizzeryl, a drow wizard who works for House Auvryndar and the Zhentarim, holds rooms here. |
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:78: - **The town:** Skullport fills an immense cavern. It has three levels of its own, a lower, a middle and a top level, which have nothing to do with Undermountain's levels. A stone bridge joins the southern end of the town to Skull Island, where the River Sargauth flows around a fortress garrisoned by Xanathar's minions.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:80: - **Who lives there:** Few people have permanent homes in Skullport, and most local businesses are shut. The ones still open pay Xanathar for protection. Adventurers can find food, drink, shelter and supplies, or a quick death if they antagonize the locals.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:83: - **The Guild's hold:** Xanathar's Guild members haunt the dilapidated buildings. Xanathar itself lives in a dungeon complex under the town, which Dragon Heist describes. The Guild controls access to Skullport's smuggling networks and gladiatorial circuits.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:89: - **Xanathar's Guild:** The Guild holds Skullport and "is trying to secure the uppermost levels of Undermountain." On Level 2 it keeps two watch posts, led by a drow named Shunn "Spider Eyes" Shurreth in the north and a human berserker named Nadia the Unbent in the south.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:90: - **Intellect devourers:** **Xanathar** is served by **Nihiloor**, which spends its days making intellect devourers and releasing them in the sewers. Dragon Heist says an intellect devourer in league with Xanathar ate **Meloon Wardragon**'s brain several months ago, and that Meloon had joined a fledgling adventuring band on an expedition into Undermountain three months before the adventure. On Level 3 a mind flayer named Ulquess, Skullport's ambassador to Stromkuhldur, implants intellect devourers in goblinoids to make spies loyal to Xanathar.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:92: - **House Auvryndar:** A low-ranking drow house from Menzoberranzan is slowly and secretly consolidating its power in Undermountain. Its invasion of Level 3 is meant as a first step toward taking Skullport. Dragon Heist holds two of its drow in Xanathar's lair, Zaibon Kyszalt, captured in Skullport, and his superior Raelyn Auvryndar, and **Nihiloor** has learned of the house's plot to conquer the town.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:98: Undermountain is the largest and deepest dungeon in the world, said to plunge as many as twenty levels beneath Waterdeep. Its best-known entrance is the Yawning Portal's well, and the mad wizard Halaster is believed to dwell below. Skullport, a grim smugglers' town in a vast cavern on the River Sargauth, connects to the dungeon's third level. Xanathar's Guild is said to rule it, outside the reach of the city's laws.
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:109: > - **Bregan D'aerthe's eyes (campaign only):** A drow woman at the Yawning Portal's balcony table "has been at that table every evening for two weeks, cataloguing who descends into Undermountain and who comes back" (`campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:29`). She is **Ryvarra**, and the Finding Floon design notes say that Jarlaxle has had eyes on the party "since their first night in the city" (`design-notes.md:11`). The company's Skullport drop in **Houseless Noble** is a ropewalk on the lower quay: "Xanathar's Guild holds the whole place, so say nothing of us in the street" (`campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/ev-01-houseless-noble.md:284`).
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:110: > - **Routes the campaign adds (campaign only):** A trapdoor under the kitchen rug at the Terasse Estate in the Castle Ward "leads to an underground dock and on to Skullport. Gladiators pass through on their way to Xanathar's lair" (`campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:89`). The Emerald Enclave keeps three sewer routes in **Autumnreaver**. The event says it plainly: "Every route runs to **Level 1** of Undermountain and no deeper" (`emerald-enclave/r10-autumnreaver/ev-01-autumnreaver.md:267`). The routes start in the Sea Ward, the Southern Ward and the Dock Ward. The Dock Ward route ends at the Grell Hideout, where the grells of **The Grells in the Dock Ward** came from after the Guild's cargo tunnel broke through.

campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:10: > - **Location journals:** **Trollskull Manor** and **Gralhund Villa** (North Ward), **Cassalanter Villa** and **House of Inspired Hands** (Sea Ward), and **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** and **Sea Maidens Faire** (Dock Ward). campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:44: | Dock Ward | The steep working port of piers, warehouses and tenements, where most of the city's poor live beside the Field Ward. | **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, the Skewered Dragon, the Seven Masks Theater, Mistshore | **Dock Ward** |
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:59: - **The sewers:** The dragonward bars dragons from the sewers as it does from the city. Watch captains hold the keys to the sewer access points of their wards, and the Field Ward has no sewers at all. Two routes matter to the campaign, the Dock Ward grate that leads to **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** and the stair in the Castle Ward sewers that leads to the lair.
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:78: - **Guild Law:** It sits outside the legal code, though the Watch and the magisters take a guild's traditions seriously. Joining is optional, but a non-member finds that guild members refuse their coin, which covers nearly everyone who sells the necessities of life. No thieves' guild is active in the city, and Xanathar's Guild is a faction with no guild standing.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:71: Several more officers have no Notable Figures page, so they stay as plain text. Hyustus Staget (Lawful Good, Illuskan Human, he/him) is the uptight captain who keeps the peace in the Dock Ward and has been dragging his heels on the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild, since he reasons that they may as well destroy each other. If he has died, the published adventure replaces him with Cressa Galavarco (Lawful Good, Tethyrian Human, she/her), a veteran. Officers Jonn Bannock (Lawful Neutral, Illuskan Human, he/him) and Ysa Terrow (Lawful Neutral, Tethyrian Human, she/her) work the late shift in **Ten Nights in the City of the Dead**, where they keep the party off the Brandath ground.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/code-legal-and-magisters.md:62: > - **Corruption and its price:** Harko Swornhold tried to bribe Magister Kylynne Silmerhelve about three years ago and was exiled, and the campaign has him back in Waterdeep and working for Xanathar's Guild in **The Exile's Return** (campaign-only). A bribe is therefore a real crime in Waterdeep, and the sentence for one can be exile. The published adventure says the City Guard must never demand a toll, and a traveler who is asked for one should report it to a magister.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:25: - **No Guild for Thieves:** The adventure says Xanathar's Guild is a faction and not an actual guild, and that despite its name it has no official guild status in Waterdeep. It appears on no guild list. See **Xanathar's Guild**.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:68: > - **The Dungsweepers' Guild:** In the published adventure, members of the guild or of Xanathar's Guild erase the chalk symbols in the sewers, and four Dungsweepers carrying intellect devourers visit the tavern on the second night of a Bregan D'aerthe renown mission. The campaign builds on both. In **The Long Watch**, Delvon Harsk sells the crew's timings to Pirn, a street agent of Xanathar's Guild, to pay a 300 gp debt, and Berna's crew is clean. In **Three Nights**, four Dungsweepers' workers sit down at the back table in the evening and say nothing. In **The Exile's Return**, a Dungsweepers' night man is one of three sources who name the rope warehouse in Mudwagon Court (campaign-only).

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:7: > - **What this is:** The Era page for the history of Xanathar's Guild and of Skullport, the undercity it rules from. It is the second of eight eras, after **Ahghairon's Gifts** and before **Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road**.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:8: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Xanathar, Ahmaergo or Nar'l Xibrindas, before answering a contact's question about where the Guild comes from, and before running area X33 of **Xanathar's Lair**, the crypt of the previous Xanathars.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:9: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Faction Outposts**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Vault of Dragons**, and the **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** location journal.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:12: **Purpose:** Lets the GM answer any question about why Xanathar is a title, how the Guild remembers or forgets its past, what Skullport is to it and what the lair's crypt proves.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:14: **Keywords:** Xanathar, Xanathar's Guild, Zushaxx, Kirukesai, Skullport, Sargauth Enclave, Netherese, Skulls of Skullport, Ahmaergo, Nar'l Xibrindas, Nihiloor, Crypt of Xanathars Past
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:18: **Other Names:** Xanathar's Guild is the campaign name. The published adventure drops the possessive and the wiki calls it Xanathar's Thieves' Guild. The wiki also gives the Agents of the Eye as the older Skullport name, used in 1372 DR. No in-world name for the era itself is sourced. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:22: > - "Xanathar" is a title held by a succession of beholders. Each holder inherits the Guild's apparatus and its paranoia together. The current Xanathar is a beholder named Zushaxx, and its pronouns are "it". Kirukesai was one of the previous holders.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:23: > - The Guild keeps continuity by eliminating anyone who knows more than the current Xanathar. The campaign line is that Zushaxx believes it is the original. The crypt in the lair shows the title is a succession, and the user has ruled that the remix line wins over the published room.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:30: - "Xanathar" is a title, derived from the personal name of the Guild's founder and held by the leaders who came after (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar (title)). The founder established the criminal underworld's structure, and each later Xanathar inherited the apparatus and the paranoia together.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:31: - The published adventure agrees that the current Xanathar is not the first beholder to claim the mantle (WDH, "Xanathar" entry).
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:32: - The current Xanathar is a beholder named Zushaxx, and its pronouns are "it". Kirukesai was one of the previous Xanathars. The wiki lists both names and spells the second Kirukeskai or Kirueskai.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:33: - The wiki's account of the first holder: a beholder hired by the Zhentarim to start a cell in Skullport. It cut them off, built its own guild, and by 1357 DR the Guild was a thieves' guild working through freelance agents under four Councilors. Each Councilor supervised one domain: mercenaries, bookkeeping, thievery or blackmail (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild; Waterdeep and the North). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:34: - The wiki's account of Kirukesai: a beholder slaver in Skullport who led the Agents of the Eye, killed the first Xanathar and took the name. It merged the two organizations without telling the rank and file, and by 1372 DR it ruled through eleven Masters. Accounts of its death differ. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:36: - The only account of how Zushaxx took the title is the wiki's: Izulktur replaced itself with Zushaxx, with no method or date given (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild and Xanathar; Dungeon #206). No campaign page adds to it.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:40: - The Guild keeps continuity by systematically eliminating anyone who knows more than the current Xanathar. What remains is a surveillance network with no institutional memory beyond what the current beholder chooses to believe.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:44: - Ahmaergo writes most of the orders that bear Xanathar's name.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:47: ### The Current Xanathar
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:49: - **Campaign line:** Zushaxx believes it is the original Xanathar. The belief is now structural to the Guild's identity and cannot be corrected. The user has settled the line: the remix overrides the published adventure, so the page needs no earlier cause for the belief (Draft Notes).
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:52: - Zushaxx also appointed four lieutenants, and the wiki names Kal'dir, Quid, Sial Sapphire and Draak. It says the four were later expanded to six (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild; Dungeon #206 and Waterdeep: Dragon Heist). None of these appears in the campaign's Notable Figures. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:56: - Skullport is a subterranean town in a vast cavern on the River Sargauth, connected to the third level of Undermountain. It has three levels of buildings, and a bridge leads from its southern end to Skull Island, where a fortress stands garrisoned by Xanathar's minions (WDMM, "Skullport").
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:59: - The published adventure lists the town's earlier occupants as duergar, drow, dwarves, crazed wizards, slavers and pirates. Today it is under the sway of Xanathar, and the businesses that remain pay the Guild for protection.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:63: - The lair is an ancient dungeon complex built by Netherese wizards and expanded by beholders over time. A magically lit tunnel of 300 feet runs from it to the Guts & Garters Inn in Skullport, and a secret staircase gives access to Waterdeep's sewers (WDH, "Xanathar's Lair").
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:70: - The war with Manshoon's Zhentarim is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward. It began when Manshoon's envoys came to Skullport three months before the fireball with Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, and Xanathar killed them.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:71: - Eye #1 now sits in Sylgar's bowl in the lair, and Xanathar does not know what it is.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:72: - Xanathar suspects Nar'l of disloyalty and has set a grell to kill him at the first proof. It does not know that he reports to Bregan D'aerthe or to Jarlaxle.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:76: Xanathar's Guild is the largest criminal organization in Waterdeep and, according to rumor, the only one led by a beholder. Its roots lie in Skullport, an outlaw port in the caverns below the city, reached through Undermountain and outside the Watch's jurisdiction, which Netherese wizards built long ago and which many masters have held since. Its informants work in every ward, and its main territory above ground is the Dock Ward. It is said that many of its own members have never seen their master.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:80: > - Nihiloor, the mind flayer, has been psionically dominating Xanathar for the last four months. The Xanathar that the party meets, including the one who killed Manshoon's envoys and sent response teams against the party's allies, has been Nihiloor's instrument. Xanathar does not know.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:81: > - Nar'l Xibrindas is a Bregan D'aerthe spy. Xanathar suspects a mole but does not know where Nar'l's reports go.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:82: > - The crypt of previous Xanathars (area X33 of **Xanathar's Lair**) shows that the title is claimed through murder. Of the Guild's own people, only Nar'l knows the room exists.

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:121: > - **Area X33, the Crypt of Xanathars Past.** The published room holds four dead beholders preserved in embalming fluid in floor-to-ceiling crystal cylinders lit from within, and nine alcoves with a copper urn each, topped by ten eyestalks. Pouring out an urn's dust makes it coalesce into a floating beholder for a few seconds, growl and fall. A carved wall shows a scowling beholder flanked by two hooded wizards. If Xanathar is present, it gazes somberly at the tombs. The remix overrides this detail: Zushaxx believes it is the original, so it does not mourn the dead.

campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:71: - Before the campaign opens, Jarlaxle has an agent inside Xanathar's Guild and the *Scarlet Marpenoth* moored in Deepwater Harbor. The Faire's carnival fleet and its parades arrive in the harbor on Ches 21 for Fleetswake, Ches 21 to 30. He arrives in Waterdeep already knowing of the Stone, the three Eyes and the vault, and Nar'l's reports keep him current. The nimblewright ledger puts him in the city no later than Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR. Eye #3 reaches him aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* on the night before Ches 1, lifted from Xanathar's hideout by agents already inside it (see **The Open Lord's Fall**).

Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse the same night and carried off what it found in the back office, the Eye included, without knowing what the Eye was. Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night, and it reached Jarlaxle aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* before the party reached the hideout. campaign/setting/history/08-the-players-arrive.md:73: Waterdeep enters the spring of 1492 DR as a prosperous city with a restless underworld. Brawls between the Xanathar's Guild and the Zhentarim have become common in the Dock Ward, and the City Watch is called to them often. Several noble households have taken delivery of mechanical servants bought from a foreign dealer, and the city is preparing for Fleetswake, the maritime festival that fills the harbor at the end of Ches.

campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:15: The Stone of Golorr is an aboleth that magic has turned into an artifact, and it holds the location of the Vault of Dragons. Dagult Neverember blinded it by removing three gemstone Eyes and putting them in three separate hands, so that a thief who stole the Stone alone would learn nothing. When the campaign opens, Xanathar holds one Eye, the Cassalanters hold one and Jarlaxle holds one.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:40: - **Eye #1, Xanathar:** Manshoon's Zhentarim stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. About three months before the fireball, Manshoon sent two lieutenants to Skullport with it as a gift. Xanathar ate them and kept the Eye, and Ott Steeltoes put it in the bowl of Sylgar, Xanathar's goldfish, because it was a pretty color. Xanathar does not know what the Eye is.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:42: - **Eye #3, Jarlaxle:** It began in Renaer's locket. Manshoon's cell seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried the Eye off with the rest of the back office, and nobody in the Guild recognized it. Bregan D'aerthe agents posing as guards lifted it from a strongbox in the hideout before dawn, and it reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:45: - **Who needs what:** Xanathar's Guild wants the Stone and does not know what the Eyes are for. The Cassalanters need the Stone and Eyes #1 and #3. Bregan D'aerthe needs the Stone and Eyes #1 and #2.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:50: - **After the vote:** Neverember's spies take the Stone from the Palace and are killed leaving the city. It passes from hand to hand until it reaches Xanathar.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:51: - **Ches 20:** Dalakhar steals it from Xanathar's lair on Neverember's orders.

campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:100: - **Xanathar's Guild:** Xanathar wants the Stone back and the vault kept hidden. It knows the Stone is the key to the vault, but it does not know what the Eye in Sylgar's bowl does, that there are three Eyes, or where the vault is.
