Samara (gladiatorial combatant, six weeks in captivity) knows: Xanathar holds "a magic stone and a marble with a colored center"; the marble is in "the fish tank in the big room with the purple carpet"; and Manshoon's organization is based in a tower in the North Ward (overheard from another prisoner since sent to the arena). The tower information is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**. Two Guild Soldiers watch the cell gate; a third is asleep in a chair. Cell locks are DC 13 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools). If all three prisoners are freed, the two Guild members in custody offer to start a fight at X2 — buying the party 5 minutes before being subdued.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:149: **X12 — Ahmaergo's Quarters.** Organized with obsessive precision: fight records, tournament schedules, arena financial ledgers, a battered sparring sword from Baldur's Gate. On the desk: *Briefing for Xanathar Concerning the Grand Game*. The document covers the current state of the Stone competition — intelligence on the Cassalanters, the Bregan D'aerthe (incomplete), and a physical description of the party accurate enough to confirm they've been watched. Ahmaergo knows who the party is.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:157: **X19 — Xanathar's Sanctum.** A circular room with purple carpet. Personal relics on shelves: small figures, collected baubles, trinkets from significant moments in Xanathar's life. At the center, Sylgar's bowl — a large crystal sphere filled with salt water, algae, decorative rocks, and Sylgar the goldfish. At the bottom of the sphere, among the rocks, glinting faintly through the water's refractions: Eye #1. A small smoky-gray gemstone Ott Steeltoes placed there months ago because it looked like it belonged.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:159: Xanathar is here. The party must extract the Eye with a paranoid beholder in the room — unless they have already used X20.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:161: Extraction without disturbing Sylgar: the sphere has a narrow top opening. DC 12 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) to remove a stone from the bottom without startling the fish. On a failure, Sylgar thrashes; the water splashes audibly. Xanathar snaps to attention: "What happened to Sylgar? What did you do to Sylgar?" If the fish is harmed — even accidentally — Xanathar becomes Hostile regardless of any prior rapport and does not negotiate. It begins with eye rays.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:163: **X20 — Dream Nullifier.** A copper bowl in a maintenance alcove between X19 and X21. Passive Perception 15 to notice while passing. Destroying it: antimagic field or dispel magic (DC 15). Effect: within 3 minutes, Xanathar experiences intrusive beholder-images and leaves X19 in agitation to find Ahmaergo — demanding explanation, seeking confirmation the lair is secure. It moves toward X18 or X12. X19 is empty. The party has approximately 4 minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:165: **X28 — Guild Recreation Hall.** No scrying sensor. Card games, arm wrestling, complaints about Xanathar. Off-duty Guild members here will not scrutinize strangers with a plausible story. The party can rest up to 1 hour, plan next steps, or wait out a patrol sweep.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:167: **X33 — Crypt of Xanathar's Past.** Through the secret door from X6 (DC 17 Perception), a narrow passage leads to a chamber containing the preserved remains and personal effects of every previous beholder who held the title "Xanathar" — predecessors stretching back centuries. Nar'l Xibrindas is the only current Guild member who knows this room exists. The room contains: evidence that "Xanathar" is a title claimed through murder, not inherited; the original Guild charter signed by the first holder; and locked coffer containing the personal journals of the last three Xanathars, including early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors. The revelation that Xanathar is a title implies the current one could be replaced, and that someone may already be planning to do so.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:169: **X35 — Nar'l's Office.** Sparse and organized by a mind that trusts nothing to memory when paper will do. The desk holds Guild operational reports and a coded communication log in Jarlaxle's cipher (DC 16 Intelligence to decode without the key). In a false-bottomed drawer (DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)): the *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* — a summary of Jarlaxle's operational activity through the theater's ownership chain, its connection to the Sea Maidens Faire, and a partial decoding of Jarlaxle's Grand Game position. Nar'l has kept this from Xanathar for eleven months. Finding it is one of three clue paths to **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:178: - **X26 — The Puppet.** A psionic relay device, currently active, maintaining a Dominate Monster effect on a psychic signature that matches Xanathar's. Detailed notes track behavioral corrections applied over four months: which tactical decisions were changed, which Guild members were elevated or removed at Nihiloor's direction, and the long-term strategic repositioning of the Guild toward Nihiloor's actual goals. The beholder who rules Xanathar's Guild does not rule Xanathar's Guild. Every Xanathar decision the party has encountered was Nihiloor's decision.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:182: Xanathar's location is pre-set per Scene 2. Its combat behavior is governed by the two-phase boss stat block in the **Bestiary** (not yet drafted): Phase 1 is the paranoid tyrant at full power; Phase 2 (below half HP) is the cornered megalomaniac — more dangerous in raw eye ray output, but less tactically focused. The ring of invisibility escape and flight toward X19 are designed into the Phase 2 transition. The party can predict this if they gathered intelligence; the **Xanathar** page in Notable Figures confirms Xanathar runs when genuinely frightened.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:184: Ahmaergo occupies X12 during non-tournament periods and patrols the X12–X18 corridor during tournaments. He never retreats while Xanathar is alive in the lair; if Xanathar falls, Ahmaergo fights to the death regardless. Nar'l is in X35 unless pre-arranged otherwise, with grell bodyguard. He covertly assists within cover constraints but will not enter open combat for the party. Nihiloor occupies X26 unless drawn by a specific threat, uses Dominate Monster on the most dangerous combatant, retreats toward X4 at half HP. Noska Ur'gray is in X8 during non-tournament periods, in X6 arena-side during games; responds to alarms with rust monster companion. Ott Steeltoes flees at first sign of violence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:186: Area-assigned: Guild Soldiers in pairs at X2, X4, X18 (doubled at Alert tier); Xanathar's Guards at X7, door detail at X18, and X19 during Xanathar's absence; Panopticus Dwarves (3) in X16, non-combatants.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:190: 30–60 civilian spectators in X2, X6, X17, and X18. Xanathar processes from X18 through X17 to X6 every 2–4 hours. Guard contingent at X6 shifts to crowd management rather than intrusion detection during active fights. Ahmaergo is in X12 reviewing records. Noska is arena-side. During the 15-minute interval between fights (arena reset, spectator movement), the panopticus sensors in X6 and X18 are trained on the arena floor and seating — not the service corridors behind the seating. This is the optimal movement window for parties using the gladiatorial transport entry.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:196: **Stage 2 — Sector Alert.** Trigger: Stage 1 response finds armed intruders, or a second alarm fires before the first resolves. Response: Noska and rust monster respond to the sector; all doors in the affected area lock (DC 15 Thieves' Tools); Xanathar is notified and moves to X18 if not already there. Response time: 2d4 minutes from Stage 1.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:198: **Stage 3 — Full Alert.** Trigger: a named NPC (Ahmaergo, Nar'l, Noska) confirms combat with intruders. Response: all off-duty Guild members in X28 and X8 mobilize; Ahmaergo leads a team toward the reported position; Xanathar retreats to X19 with four Guild Soldiers. Response time: 1d6 minutes from Stage 2.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:200: **Stage 4 — Xanathar Engaged.** Trigger: Xanathar is directly threatened or Sylgar is disturbed. Response: Xanathar deploys eye rays without tactical prioritization; Ahmaergo abandons his team position and moves directly to X19; the lair's entire combat-capable population converges. The only exits are through them, past them, or under cover of the Jarlaxle distraction.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:223: | Party in X19 when Jarlaxle arrives | Three-way: PCs, Jarlaxle, Xanathar (present unless X20 was used) | Jarlaxle immediately negotiates — he'd rather split benefit than fight. His offer: the Eye for a favor to be named later. He does not negotiate down from this |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:238: **Route 3 — Undermountain Level 1.** Emergency use only. The maintenance tunnel through X4 connects to Undermountain Level 1 approximately 600 feet below the lair. The party is safe from Xanathar, but now in Undermountain, which has its own conditions. This route is a designed fail-forward path: the campaign continues into the Undermountain premise prematurely, which is a consequence the party should understand before choosing it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:280: **Xanathar's Guild:** Record one of three Xanathar Event Outcomes:
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:282: - **Xanathar Intact** (Xanathar alive, Eye taken): It doesn't know immediately what was stolen. It notices Sylgar's bowl was disturbed within hours. When it identifies what is missing — days of interrogation later — its response will be disproportionate. The party is now personally known to Xanathar. This intensifies **Vault of Dragons**'s difficulty.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:283: - **Xanathar Wounded** (Xanathar alive, fought and fled): As above, plus Ahmaergo takes over lair security. Xanathar is less effective in **Vault of Dragons** but more enraged.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:284: - **Xanathar Gone** (lair demolished or Xanathar killed): Guild collapses into Ahmaergo-led factional fighting. Response teams disbanded. Nihiloor escapes to Undermountain Level 17. The party permanently loses Guild intelligence as a resource. **Vault of Dragons**'s approach routes through former Guild contacts who are now independent actors.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:286: **Manshoon's Zhentarim Splinter.** Monitoring via agents watching the lair's entrances. If Xanathar is weakened or eliminated, Manshoon moves within days to absorb abandoned Guild territory. His alert tier increases if the party was observed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:299: - **Tashlyn or Davil:** "Is Xanathar still operational?" Direct and tactical — the Doom Raiders' expansion into former Guild territory depends on the answer. "And Ahmaergo?"
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:316: | 4–6 | **Xanathar's Lair** | The party recovers the Eye of the Stone from Xanathar's Lair | 4 |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:318: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:337: - **Faction Outposts**, Scene 4 (Xanathar Outposts) — Terasse Estate, Grinda Garloth's, Orb Confectioners. Entry methods seeded in **Faction Outposts** are executed in Scene 2.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:339: - **Notable Figures** — Full NPC profiles for Xanathar, Ahmaergo, Nar'l Xibrindas, Nihiloor, Noska Ur'gray, Ott Steeltoes, Thorvin Twinbeard, Keln Trigos, Grinda Garloth, Korgstrod Uxgulm, Gorath.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:340: - **Bestiary** (not yet drafted) — Custom two-phase boss stat block for Xanathar. Custom stat blocks for Ahmaergo (minotaur veteran), Nar'l (drow mage + grell bodyguard), Nihiloor (mind flayer arcanist).
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:341: - the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page — Xanathar's Guild faction overview and NPC roster. **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page — outpost entries, response team compositions, escalation framework, Revelation List, Nihiloor's three-project breakdown.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:345: - **Vault of Dragons** — the Xanathar Event Outcome (**Xanathar Intact**, **Xanathar Wounded** or **Xanathar Gone**) recorded in Scene 6 Aftermath affects **Vault of Dragons**'s difficulty and approach options.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:350: # Design Notes: Xanathar's Lair
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:368: Xanathar does not know the Eye's value. Ott Steeltoes thought it was a pretty stone and placed it where Xanathar likes its fish's habitat to look interesting. It has been sitting there for months, protected less by paranoid security than by the fact that nobody knows it's there. This placement tells the party more about Xanathar's nature in one beat than any amount of descriptive prose: a being of tremendous power who keeps a world-altering artifact as an inadvertent pet decoration.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:370: Xanathar is in X19 when the party arrives. This is not conditional on tier or timing — it is the design. The question is not whether Xanathar is there but whether the party has found X20.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:372: A party that found the dream nullifier through Thorvin's guidance or onsite reconnaissance has a tool: destroy it, wait three minutes, follow Xanathar out of the room, and extract the Eye with four minutes of silence. A party that didn't find X20 must extract the Eye while a paranoid beholder watches them. Both paths reach the same moment — the Sleight of Hand check against the fishbowl — but the first is deliberate and timed, and the second is improvised and desperate. This is the correct two-track design for a heist arc: preparation should matter, and the party that prepared should feel it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:374: Making the rule that harming Sylgar turns Xanathar immediately and irreversibly Hostile is correct: it reflects that Xanathar values Sylgar above guild loyalty, above tactical self-interest, above anything else in the lair. Violating that should carry the most severe possible consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:400: The case for it is real: a villain faction eliminated, a dangerous location made safe, a complicated enemy relationship resolved with finality. The case against it is also real: permanent loss of Guild intelligence, Nihiloor freed from his puppet's leash and operating unconstrained from Undermountain, Nar'l and Thorvin killed unless the party explicitly evacuates them, the Undermountain connection severed before the campaign descends. The party that destroys the lair is trading a faction they understand — paranoid, predictable, with known structures and exploitable NPCs — for an unknown quantity in Nihiloor, who is smarter than Xanathar, does not have Xanathar's attachment to a goldfish, and has been running the Guild in secret for months without anyone noticing.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:408: The Puppet revelation is the most significant: Xanathar has been Nihiloor's instrument for four months. Every aggressive, paranoid, apparently irrational decision attributed to the beholder in **Faction Outposts** and this quest was Nihiloor's decision. This changes the party's understanding retroactively. The mastermind they've been treating as the main threat was itself controlled by something worse. The real threat from Xanathar's Guild faction was never Xanathar.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:412: ## Xanathar's Boss Stat Block
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:414: Xanathar uses a custom two-phase boss stat block rather than the 2024 Monster Manual beholder entry. The two-phase structure reflects its psychological collapse under direct threat: Phase 1 is the paranoid tyrant at full power; Phase 2 (below half HP) is the cornered megalomaniac — more dangerous in raw output, less tactically controlled. The ring of invisibility escape and flight toward X19 are designed into the Phase 2 transition, not left to GM improvisation. A party that gathered intelligence knows to expect this; the **Xanathar** page in Notable Figures confirms Xanathar runs when genuinely frightened.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:416: The purpose of the two-phase design is to give Xanathar a combat identity that reflects character rather than just capability. A beholder is already terrifying by the numbers. The specific flavor of Xanathar's fight — the specific eye rays it prioritizes, the moment it breaks and runs, what it screams when it runs — comes from what it is. That specificity distinguishes this fight from any other beholder encounter the party might have.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:428: **Emerald Enclave.** Before the party enters Xanathar's lair, Jeryth Phaulkon provides an enchanted herb sprig that functions as a mild psychic shield. The holder gains advantage on the first saving throw made against an implanted intellect devourer, usable once. Jeryth asks that it be carried into the lair specifically — the enchantment is keyed to the environment's psychic signature and does not function elsewhere. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Emerald Enclave** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:430: **Order of the Gauntlet.** Savra Belabranta considers the use of intellect devourers in Xanathar's Guild a specific theological offense — aberrant intelligence overwriting natural souls. She offers to accompany the party into Xanathar's lair as her Renown 3 companion benefit (once per quest), but only if the party commits to eliminating Nihiloor permanently rather than bypassing him. If that commitment cannot be made, she does not join. The offer is genuine; so is the condition. Nihiloor always escapes, so she asks for the attempt, and she stays with the party when he gets away. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Order of the Gauntlet** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:27: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Cassalanter interactions into operational knowledge of the villa. Each faction contact provides villa-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party discovered in earlier quests. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair**'s dungeon-focused briefing, this briefing is social and political — the target looks normal from the outside.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:33: The intelligence here is shallower than **Xanathar's Lair**'s — no faction has an embedded agent inside the villa, and the Cassalanters' social position makes direct surveillance difficult. What the contacts provide is context, suspicion, and operational windows.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:263: This quest's defining mechanic. The social-layer equivalent of **Xanathar's Lair**'s alarm cascade.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:329: **If this is the first Eye restored (neither Xanathar's Lair nor Sea Maidens Faire completed).** The Stone awakens for the first time. The attuned character hears a voice — old and precise, like water that has been underground for a long time. It does not say hello. "Two remain." No elaboration. The fragmentary images from **Faction Outposts**, Scene 1 clarify: "salt air and creaking wood, dark-skinned elf" resolves to a ship in Waterdeep harbor, a theater company's banner — a bald elf at the prow, patient, watching. "An eye, unblinking in darkness, carved stone wet from underground springs" resolves to the beholder's lair beneath Skullport. Unprompted: "I want to be whole." The Stone's cooperation with the party is overlap of interest, not loyalty.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:331: **If this is the second Eye restored (one of Xanathar's Lair or Sea Maidens Faire completed).** The Stone's voice deepens. It becomes more communicative — almost conversational, still alien and cold. New disclosures:
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:334: - The remaining Eye's location resolves fully. Which impression sharpens depends on which quest the party completed first. If **Xanathar's Lair** was first (Xanathar's lair already raided), the final impression is: "A ship in the harbor. The Scarlet Marpenoth. A bald elf with a year of patience." If **Sea Maidens Faire** was first (Jarlaxle's Eye already recovered), the final impression is: "Beneath Skullport. An eye, unblinking in carved stone, wet from underground springs. It has been waiting longer than anyone has been looking." The Stone identifies each Eye by proximity-sense, not by name.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:339: **If this is the third Eye restored (both Xanathar's Lair and Sea Maidens Faire already completed).** The Stone goes still, then speaks with sudden, precise clarity. The fragmentary impressions are gone. The vault's location, the command phrase, and the nature of its guardian resolve in full. Run the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons** at this point. Quest order does not change the awakening, only its timing.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:355: **Xanathar's Guild.** If **Xanathar's Lair** already completed, no change. If not, Xanathar's awareness of the party's activities depends on imp surveillance from C25 — if the villa was assaulted rather than infiltrated, reports of unusual activity near the Sea Ward reach the Guild's informant network.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and 1–2 Eyes. The children's fate is resolved or ticking. The remaining lair heists — whichever of **Xanathar's Lair**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** have not been completed — are available in any order. The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (C6/C22) points toward **Kolat Towers**. Esvele's Viper Den (if discovered) points toward the Brandath Crypts (**Vault of Dragons**). The Stone's impressions guide the party toward remaining targets.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:399: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory aftermath — the Stone speaks for the first time — this quest's aftermath is about what the party chose. The children, the Cassalanters, the evidence, the poison. This quest's resolution is moral, not tactical. What the party did matters more than what they found.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:411: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:432: - **Xanathar's Lair, Scene 6** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (Sea Ward villa, hidden temple, Cassalanter house mark) point toward this quest. Cassalanter faction state set in **Xanathar's Lair** reflects awareness of the party's activities.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:437: - **Kolat Towers** — The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (C6/C22) is the third of three independent clue paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony (**Xanathar's Lair** X7) and Nihiloor's files (**Xanathar's Lair** X23). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied across quests.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:439: - **Undermountain (future)** — Asmodean retribution from Path 2 (contract destruction) seeds Vanrakdoom, Level 18. The Stone's 2-Eye upgrade intensifies the Mad Mage seed from **Xanathar's Lair**. Osvaldo's freed soul may appear in the afterlife mechanics.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:447: This quest must feel like social infiltration followed by dungeon horror, and the distinction from **Xanathar's Lair** (dungeon infiltration), **Sea Maidens Faire** (caper heist), and **Kolat Towers** (fortress raid) is structural, not cosmetic. The social exposure system — Accepted, Noticed, Suspected, Exposed — is the defining mechanic above ground. It creates a different kind of tension than the panopticus: the panopticus watches through magic; the villa watches through people. A failed Stealth check in **Xanathar's Lair** triggers an alarm cascade. A failed Deception check in this quest triggers a social escalation that the party can still recover from — but each recovery narrows their options.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:473: The parallel heist mirrors Jarlaxle's simultaneous operation in **Xanathar's Lair**, with three structural functions.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:499: The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is placed in both C6 (Victoro's desk) and C22 (Ammalia's study). This dual placement ensures that a party reaching either room — not necessarily both — discovers the path to Kolat Towers. The report references Manshoon by operational name, identifies the Towers' location, describes Zhentarim splinter activity, and notes the force-field entry restriction. It is the third of three independent paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony from **Xanathar's Lair** X7 and Nihiloor's intelligence files from **Xanathar's Lair** X23.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:501: The Three Clue Rule is now satisfied across quests. A party that skipped **Xanathar's Lair** entirely can still reach **Kolat Towers** through this quest. A party that completed **Xanathar's Lair** but missed both the Samara and Nihiloor paths can reach **Kolat Towers** through the Cassalanter report. No single failed check, missed room, or skipped quest blocks all three paths. This is the correct application of the Three Clue Rule in a campaign where lair heists run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:505: The four-tier difficulty table translates **Faction Outposts** outpost consequences into this quest's environment with a harsher progression than **Xanathar's Lair**. At Alert tier, the social entry window closes entirely — no charity ball, no Goldenight gala. The party must create their own access. At Lockdown, the social infiltration genre is no longer possible — this quest's top layer collapses into a different kind of operation.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:507: This is more severe than **Xanathar's Lair**'s scaling (where higher tiers added guards and traps but did not change the fundamental approach) because social infiltration depends on the target being socially open. A villa that is closed to visitors cannot be socially infiltrated. The party that hit both Cassalanter outposts in **Faction Outposts** traded social access for intelligence — a real cost that the difficulty table makes legible.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:525: The 2-Eye disclosure is written as a progression from the 1-Eye awakening in **Xanathar's Lair**. The Stone becomes more communicative — almost conversational — because the restored Eye returns capacity. The final Eye's location resolves fully: the Scarlet Marpenoth, a bald elf, a year of patience. The Vault's existence is acknowledged without detail. The aboleth consciousness bleeds through more strongly.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:527: The conditional handling (this is Eye #1 vs. Eye #2) ensures the scene works regardless of quest order. A party that runs **Cassalanter Villa** before **Xanathar's Lair** gets the 1-Eye awakening here; a party that runs **Xanathar's Lair** first gets the 2-Eye upgrade. Both are written in full. The GM does not need to interpolate.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:15: The Eye had reached him the night before Ches 1, through Bregan D'aerthe intelligence work — the same network that had placed Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar's Guild a year earlier, in 1491. Jarlaxle understood the Eye's significance better than any other faction in the Grand Game, which is why he was not rushing. He would watch. He would learn who else was moving on the Stone. If the party pursuing the Stone proved competent, he would engage them on his own terms. If they did not, he would take the Eye himself when the moment was right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:25: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Jarlaxle interactions into actionable fleet knowledge. Each faction contact provides ship-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party already discovered. Same format as **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 1 and **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 1: a montage of faction consultations, each adding a piece to the picture.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:31: Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (where the briefing confirms a known dungeon) or **Cassalanter Villa** (where the briefing describes a villa that looks normal from outside), this briefing reveals that the target is itself a deception. Every contact is pointing at the same fleet, but none of them fully understand what it is. The party should leave this scene feeling that they are casing something that does not want to be cased — a carnival that is also a military installation, operated by people who have not been found out by any of them.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:47: **Tone.** Convergent intelligence with a note of uncertainty. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (where the target is a known dungeon) or **Cassalanter Villa** (where the target looks normal from outside), this briefing reveals that the target is a deception — and the party must decide how deep the deception runs before they can plan against it. Every contact is pointing at the same fleet, but none of them fully understand what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:57: *Identifying the Score.* Eye #3 is aboard the Scarlet Marpenoth, in Jarlaxle's stateroom (U4), sitting in a velvet-lined compartment built into the base of the crystal ball pedestal. The Stone's impression (if 1+ Eyes restored) sharpens to: "a ship in the harbor, the Scarlet Marpenoth, a bald elf with a year of patience." **Faction Outposts** documents — Fenerus Stormcastle's decoded messages confirming intelligence flows to the Sea Maidens Faire, Malkolm Brizzenbright's ghost testimony that "Rongquan Mystere" met with drow who mentioned the Faire — converge on the fleet. **Xanathar's Lair** documents — the Report on Seven Masks Theater from X35 — provide a third confirmation path.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:87: **Tone.** Reconnaissance with a carnival veneer. The party is casing a target that looks like fun — bright colors, music, crowds, exotic spectacles. Beneath the surface, the same methodical intelligence-gathering as **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa**, but dressed in greasepaint. The contrast between the carnival atmosphere and the covert military operation underneath is the scene's defining texture.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:168: **U3 (Soluun's Stateroom).** Soluun Xibrindas is present during standard operating hours and at night; absent during parade windows (he uses the crowds for stalking). Contains: a Letter from Nar'l Xibrindas confirming the BD mole in Xanathar's organization and mentioning the Seven Masks Theater purchase. Soluun is the most dangerous combatant aboard the submarine — volatile, cruel, and fanatically loyal to Jarlaxle. He attacks intruders without hesitation and without the non-lethal restraint other BD operatives show. If the party contains an elf or half-elf, Soluun fixates on them.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:184: *The Three-Beat Improvisation Structure.* This quest does not use **Xanathar's Lair**'s four-stage alarm cascade or **Cassalanter Villa**'s four-stage social exposure system. Instead:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:253: **If this is Eye #1 (neither Xanathar's Lair nor Cassalanter Villa completed).** The Stone awakens for the first time. A voice — neither masculine nor feminine, faintly resonant with something older than the gemstone itself — speaks for the first time: "Two remain." The fragmentary impressions clarify into two distinct images: a beholder's unblinking eye in deep darkness, carved stone wet from underground springs; and a Sea Ward villa with a sun symbol marking a hidden door, the Cassalanter house mark pressed into the stone above a spiral stair. The Stone does not reveal vault location, command phrase, or the nature of its own consciousness. It says "Two remain," and then it is quiet.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:255: **If this is Eye #2 (one prior lair heist completed — either Xanathar's Lair or Cassalanter Villa).** The Stone's voice deepens, and the quality of its attention changes. It is more present. It says "One remains," and then it does something it has not done before — it describes what it knows. The final Eye's location resolves fully:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:257: - *If **Xanathar's Lair** was completed first:* "A villa in the Sea Ward. A temple beneath it, stone floor engraved with the Cassalanter house mark. The children's laughter has a hollow quality — it comes from a room that has been sealed for three years."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:262: **If this is Eye #3 (both Xanathar's Lair and Cassalanter Villa already completed).** The Stone goes still. Then it speaks with sudden, precise clarity — the fragmentary impressions are gone. The vault's location does not resolve as an address but as a riddle in the Stone's alien cadence — imagery and verse pointing toward the Brandath Crypts, requiring interpretation to reach. The guardian's nature is described with the clinical precision of a being that has watched a gold dragon stand alone in the dark with five hundred thousand gold coins for years: bound by oath, loyal, patient beyond mortal reckoning, extraordinarily dangerous.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:280: **Xanathar's Guild.** If **Xanathar's Lair** already completed, no change. If not, Xanathar's awareness depends on whether Guild patrols in the Dock Ward observed harbor activity.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:302: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone with 1–3 Eyes. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) points toward **Cassalanter Villa** if not yet completed. The Letter from Nar'l (U3) confirms the BD/Xanathar connection, providing context for **Xanathar's Lair**. Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (if recovered from Scene 5) both point toward **Kolat Towers**. If this was Eye #3 — the Full Awakening — **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. The caper's resolution carries a different emotional register than **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory awakening or **Cassalanter Villa**'s moral reckoning. This quest's aftermath is about relationships — with Jarlaxle (enemy, rival, or ally?), with Zelifarn (the crystal ball decision), with Manshoon's Zhentarim (confirmed threat, now owed payback). The party has engaged with the most charming villain in the Grand Game. What that engagement produced depends entirely on how they played it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:316: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:340: - **Xanathar's Lair** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (ship in harbor, bald elf) point toward this quest. Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist in **Xanathar's Lair** shapes this quest's stakes. Report on Seven Masks Theater (X35) provides third clue path to this quest.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:352: This quest must feel like a caper, not a dungeon infiltration (**Xanathar's Lair**) or a social infiltration into a dungeon (**Cassalanter Villa**). The distinction is mechanical, not just aesthetic.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:354: **Xanathar's Lair** rewards careful routing and surveillance suborning. **Cassalanter Villa** rewards maintaining cover and reading social cues. This quest rewards improvisation — the plan will go wrong, and the party's ability to adapt in the moment determines success. The three-beat improvisation structure (Something Goes Wrong → Escalation → Confrontation or Clean Escape) replaces **Xanathar's Lair**'s four-stage alarm cascade and **Cassalanter Villa**'s four-stage social exposure system. The caper genre does not use staged escalation because the drama comes from the moment of improvisation, not from a slowly tightening noose.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:360: This quest is the only lair heist that offers three fundamentally different approaches to obtaining the Eye. This is because Jarlaxle is the only villain who would willingly trade it for something he values more. Xanathar does not negotiate — it eats envoys. The Cassalanters are too desperate — the children's deadline consumes their attention. Manshoon holds no Eye. Jarlaxle is the one Grand Game villain who treats every interaction as a transaction, and the Eye is currency he is willing to spend if the price is right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:370: Xanathar confronted is a lethal puzzle — eye rays, lair actions, raw destructive power. The Cassalanters confronted are a moral crisis — desperate parents, infernal contracts, children between the party and the door. Jarlaxle confronted is a conversation. He is faster, better informed, better equipped, and more experienced than the party. He also does not want to fight them. He wants to understand them, evaluate them, and determine whether they are more useful as allies than as enemies.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:386: Every lair heist needs a parallel heist actor: **Xanathar's Lair** has Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, **Cassalanter Villa** has Esvele Rosznar, this quest has Manshoon's Zhentarim strike team. The parallel actor creates three-way pressure, provides a pressure-relief valve, and connects the current quest to future quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:394: Every lair heist has a moral dimension: **Xanathar's Lair** has the smokepowder choice, **Cassalanter Villa** has the children's fate and the four resolution paths. This quest's moral dimension is smaller in scope but personal — the Nimblewright Crystal Ball in U4 belonged to Zelifarn's mother, killed by Jarlaxle's agents.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:408: If this quest delivers the third Eye, this is the campaign's pivotal revelation. The Stone delivers its revelation as riddle and impression, not as an address: the Brandath Crypts, a patient and unnamed guardian, imagery requiring interpretation rather than a key. The Stone speaks the command phrase here, with the vault's location and the nature of its guardian. This moment must be given its full weight regardless of which quest delivers it — the text frames the Full Awakening identically whether the third Eye came from Xanathar's fishbowl, the Asmodeus statue, or Jarlaxle's crystal ball pedestal.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:416: This quest's departure deadline (Tarsakh 20, or Tarsakh 15 at Lockdown) is unique among lair heists. Neither **Xanathar's Lair** nor **Cassalanter Villa** has a hard deadline tied to the target physically leaving the campaign's geography. The departure deadline creates urgency of a different kind than **Cassalanter Villa**'s Founders' Day: not "something bad happens if you wait" but "the opportunity disappears." The fleet sails. The Eye goes with it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:424: **To Xanathar's Lair (if not yet completed).** The Letter from Nar'l Xibrindas (U3) confirms the BD mole in Xanathar's organization and mentions the Seven Masks Theater purchase. This is one of three independent paths to **Xanathar's Lair** — connecting the BD intelligence network to Xanathar's Guild and providing operational context for the approach.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:11: Lady Gondafrey, then a City Watch captain and a knight of Tyr, attempted to breach the towers in 1379 DR while investigating Duhlark's suspected dealings with Xanathar's Guild. The force field repelled her. The arcane lock on the front door defeated her thieves. She attempted a third entry through the bridge on the third floor and was captured. Duhlark offered her a choice: remain imprisoned, or swear never to speak of what she had seen. She refused the oath. She has been in the towers since 1379 DR — 113 years.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:25: **Purpose.** Convert prior-arc intelligence — the name "Manshoon," the Kolat Towers location, the pass-amulet system, the Splinter network — into an operational picture of a fortified position. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon whose defenses the party can infiltrate around) or **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with social cover) or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival that looks like something other than a military installation), this briefing reveals a target that is overtly what it is: a wizard's fortress behind an impenetrable force field. The party cannot sneak up on Kolat Towers. They can only prepare better or worse for the assault.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:33: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Harper agents have been watching the Kolat Towers block since **Faction Outposts**, tracking lieutenant movement. Mirt provides: exterior description and confirmed ward location (Southern Ward, not the North Ward — the misdirection in Samara's **Xanathar's Lair** testimony was fragmentary field intelligence); pass-amulet delivery patterns (Manshoon's couriers approach from two directions, suggesting two off-site entry points); the observation that the force field extends twelve feet above the roof and six feet below street level, making aerial approaches impossible and undermining impossible without specialized tools; and a warning that Watch patrol coverage in the surrounding streets has been quietly reduced over the past eighteen months — someone with civic influence arranged it. At renown 10+, Mirt activates a 48-hour Harper surveillance rotation: a field agent (spy stat block) delivers a written report covering patrol patterns by time of day, delivery schedules, and one verified force-field gap cycle including the gap's precise location, duration, and rotation frequency.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:53: **Purpose.** Steps 1–3 of the heist framework adapted for the raid genre. Kolat Towers does not offer the same casing texture as **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon with accessible entry points to scout), **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with a public face), or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival in which the party can wander freely). The force field makes exterior casing the only option. The party cannot see inside the towers before the operation begins. What they can learn is: entry timing, approach windows, and force-field gap mechanics. The Identifying the Score step is unusual in this quest — no Eye, no Stone impression — which means the party's motivation must come from somewhere else entirely.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:60: - Samara's testimony (**Xanathar's Lair**, X7): "a tower in the North Ward" — deliberate misdirection from fragmentary field intelligence. The ward is wrong but the target is right.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:61: - Nihiloor's intelligence files (**Xanathar's Lair**, X23): partial Kolat Towers layout and Splinter lieutenant roster.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:283: **Tone.** Tactical decompression. **Kolat Towers**'s aftermath has a different texture than the prior lair heists' endings: no revelatory awakening (**Xanathar's Lair**), no moral reckoning (**Cassalanter Villa**), no relationship reassessment (**Sea Maidens Faire**). This is strategic positioning — the party has done a violent thing that was necessary, has recovered intelligence that changes the **Vault of Dragons** situation, and has marked a Manshoon Event Outcome that will determine what they face at the Vault. Professional satisfaction or grim assessment, depending on how the operation went.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:295: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:314: - **Xanathar's Lair**, X7 (Samara's Testimony) — "a tower in the North Ward" — deliberate misdirection, correct target wrong ward.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:315: - **Xanathar's Lair**, X23 (Nihiloor's Intelligence Files) — partial Kolat Towers layout and lieutenant roster. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:335: **Kolat Towers** is a raid — the fourth and final genre in the lair heist sequence. **Xanathar's Lair** was dungeon infiltration (stealth and navigation). **Cassalanter Villa** was social infiltration (identity and exposure management). **Sea Maidens Faire** was a caper (improvisation and rolling adaptation). **Kolat Towers** is a raid: the party assaults a fortified position with overwhelming violence, and the primary strategic variable is speed. The reinforcement timer is not a complication added to the raid — it is the genre's defining mechanic. Every quest in the sequence tests different party capabilities. **Kolat Towers** tests combat effectiveness and decision-making under time pressure.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:339: Four quests, four genres, because the party has four different capability sets that a single-genre sequence cannot test. The raid placement at the end of the sequence is deliberate: by **Kolat Towers**, the party has demonstrated stealth capability (**Xanathar's Lair**), social intelligence (**Cassalanter Villa**), and improvisational flexibility (**Sea Maidens Faire**). The raid asks whether they can leverage all of those in a context where they cannot afford to use any of them thoughtfully — speed collapses the time for planning, and the reinforcement timer punishes hesitation. A party that treats the raid like a dungeon crawl (clearing every room before advancing) loses to the timer. A party that treats it like a caper (improvising room by room without a plan) arrives at Manshoon underpowered. The raid rewards parties who have internalized the prior quests' lessons and can execute without pausing to plan.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:343: **Kolat Towers** is the only lair heist without an Eye recovery. The structural risk is that the scene feels lower-stakes than **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, or **Sea Maidens Faire** — that without the Stone's awakening, the climax lacks weight. The intelligence haul addresses this by giving each recovered item a specific, named **Vault of Dragons** consequence that the party understands before they enter the towers. The blackmail ledger is not "valuable treasure" — it is the political tool that neutralizes Manshoon's Masked Lord influence. The Report on the Grand Game is not "lore" — it contains the partial command phrase that opens the Vault. Every item in the haul has a downstream consequence that the party can predict, which makes recovering it feel as meaningful as restoring an Eye.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:345: The Manshoon Identity Reveal replaces the Stone's awakening as the quest's dramatic centerpiece. Where the Stone awakening in prior quests revealed something about the campaign's hidden architecture (Nihiloor's domination in **Xanathar's Lair**, the Cassalanters' debt in **Cassalanter Villa**, the aboleth consciousness in **Sea Maidens Faire**), the Identity Reveal reveals something about the antagonist's nature that recontextualizes every Manshoon interaction the party has had since **Faction Outposts**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:363: The three-quest reveal structure — Name in **Faction Outposts**, Identity in **Kolat Towers**, Motivation in **Vault of Dragons** — treats Manshoon differently than the other three villain faction leaders. Xanathar is immediately legible; Jarlaxle makes himself legible on his own terms; the Cassalanters are legible once the party reaches their temple. Manshoon is the one faction leader whose true nature requires assembly from distributed evidence, because his true nature is genuinely strange — a clone of a 200-year-old wizard, not just a Zhentarim leader — and because understanding it changes the party's strategic relationship with him in a way that understanding the other faction leaders does not.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:369: Each lair heist has a moment that asks the party to weigh a consequence against a tactical advantage: smokepowder demolition in **Xanathar's Lair**, the children's sacrifice in **Cassalanter Villa**, Zelifarn's crystal ball in **Sea Maidens Faire**. **Kolat Towers**'s equivalent is the Retributive Strike Gambit. If the party is winning the fight against Manshoon, they must decide how to prevent him from breaking the staff — which means either disarming him while he's still dangerous enough to threaten the gambit, or accepting the explosion, or finding a third path (negotiation that takes him past his desperation threshold without combat reaching 30 HP). The gambit compresses **Kolat Towers**'s moral dimension into a single decision point because the raid genre does not leave space for extended moral deliberation.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:19: As the Stone's third Eye clicks into its socket — in whichever lair delivered it — every surviving player in the Grand Game receives the same piece of information at the same time: the vault is real, it is beneath the Brandath Mausoleum, and it can now be opened. Xanathar, if it survives, prefers the gold stay buried and the Stone stay lost — a vault it cannot reach is still leverage it can threaten to use. The Cassalanters, depending on how the party left them, either wait on a promise or plan to take by force what they were once willing to earn. Manshoon, who holds no Eye, has been counting on exactly this moment — he intends to let someone else do the work of opening the vault and then take it from whoever succeeds. And in Neverwinter, word of a Waterdeep in open faction war over a half-million gold pieces has already reached a man who has been waiting years for a reason to come home. Dagult Neverember is packing.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:33: | Eyes recovered | **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire** | Should be 3/3 entering **Vault of Dragons**. If fewer than 3, **Vault of Dragons** cannot yet be run — see Design Notes. |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:35: | Xanathar | **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 6 | **Xanathar Intact** / **Xanathar Wounded** / **Xanathar Gone** |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:57: *The Stone's Role Going Forward.* The Full Awakening already happened — see below. What the Stone does for the remainder of **Vault of Dragons** is different in character from what it did in **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, and **Sea Maidens Faire**. It is no longer withholding information; it has nothing left to reveal about the vault itself. What remains is presence. Illuun's consciousness, fully surfaced since the third Eye was seated, is close enough now that the attuned holder occasionally catches something that is not quite communication — an impression, a mood, a flicker of attention that does not belong to the Stone's usual cadence. This should be used sparingly across Scenes 2 through 4: a handful of brief, unsettling asides rather than a running commentary, timed to moments of genuine significance (the vault doors opening, the first sight of the gold) rather than every scene transition. The Stone is not the protagonist of this quest's climax. It is a passenger who has started, very quietly, to make its presence known.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:59: *The Full Awakening (Canonical Scene).* This is the scene that **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa** defer to explicitly — both read, verbatim, "Run the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons** at this point." It already happened, off-page relative to **Vault of Dragons**'s own opening, in whichever quest delivered the third Eye. Use the following text at that point in whichever quest actually runs it; it should not be restaged here.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:195: | Xanathar's Guild | **Xanathar Intact** (Xanathar alive, Eye taken) | Xanathar itself directing an elite Guild bodyguard complement, with Ahmaergo at its side | Reclaim the Stone and punish the party — its presence makes this a vendetta as much as a retrieval |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:196: | Xanathar's Guild | **Xanathar Wounded** (Xanathar injured, absent) | Ahmaergo leading a handful of Guild agents — opportunistic rather than personal | Recover the Stone if possible; deny the gold to rivals; no escalation beyond what Ahmaergo judges recoverable |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:197: | Xanathar's Guild | **Xanathar Gone** (Xanathar dead) | Ahmaergo arrives alone or with one lieutenant, without Xanathar's authority behind him — he is here for himself, not the Guild | Seize enough gold to fund his own power grab; Guild loyalty among the remaining agents is uncertain and will not hold through a serious fight |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:212: If more than one hostile faction is present simultaneously, they do not coordinate with each other — Manshoon, Xanathar's remnants, and an Active-Adversarial Cassalanter team are all here for the same gold and are as much a threat to one another as to the party. A party that recognizes this can let its enemies do some of the fighting for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:292: | 3 (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire** — Kolat Towers skipped) | 22 | 6 |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:295: A party can only reach **Vault of Dragons** with at least three lair heists done, since all three Eyes (from **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) are required to open the Vault — Kolat Towers carries no Eye and is never required for entry, only for the fourth heist slot that unlocks level 7 early. This is why the quest is written for a 6th-to-7th-level party rather than a fixed 7th — Scene 5's Faction Confrontation in particular should be run with the entering level in mind (see that scene's roster-assembly table), since a level 6 party that skipped Kolat Towers also faces a full-strength, unidentified Manshoon by default (**Kolat Towers** is what typically resolves that threat).
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:323: - **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 6 / **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 6 — both defer explicitly to "the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons**." Scene 1's Full Awakening text is that scene.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:339: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each tested a different genre and a different set of party capabilities — dungeon infiltration, social infiltration, caper improvisation, raid violence. **Vault of Dragons** is not a fifth genre in that sequence. It is the point where all four genres' consequences arrive at once. The party does not case the vault, because there is nothing left to case — they already know where it is and what guards it. What replaces the heist framework's dramatic engine (plan, execute, adapt) is accumulated consequence: every faction relationship, every renown level, every choice about who to trust and who to betray, all resolving simultaneously. A GM running **Vault of Dragons** should not look for a new structural trick. The trick was already played, nine times, across the quests that came before it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:343: This is the single most important practical note in the document. **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa** both defer to "the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons**" without knowing which lair will actually deliver the third Eye — the party's own choices determine that. **Sea Maidens Faire**, published earlier in the drafting sequence, wrote the disclosure inline rather than deferring, on the theory that a scene this significant deserved to exist wherever it naturally lands rather than as a forward reference to a document that didn't exist yet at the time. The result is a minor structural seam: **Sea Maidens Faire**'s inline text and this document's canonical text needed to agree closely enough that a GM reading both back to back would not notice a difference. They do. If **Vault of Dragons** is being run and the third Eye came from **Xanathar's Lair** or **Cassalanter Villa**, use Scene 1's Full Awakening text as written — it is the canonical version, and it was written to match **Sea Maidens Faire**'s language precisely so that whichever quest actually delivers the disclosure, the moment reads identically. Do not restage the disclosure a second time in **Vault of Dragons**'s own Scene 1 if it already happened in Scene 6 of whichever prior quest delivered the third Eye — reference that it happened, briefly, and move on.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:347: Every prior lair heist ends its Scene 6 by marking one or two Event Outcomes for later use. **Vault of Dragons** is the quest where all of them get read at once. The Faction-State Summary Table in Scene 1 exists so a GM does not have to flip back through four other documents to reconstruct what's true — it is meant to be filled in once, before the session, and referenced throughout. The table's most important function is making Scene 5's roster assembly mechanical rather than improvised: a GM who sees **Manshoon Alive** and **Xanathar Wounded** marked and the Cassalanters Active-Adversarial can build the vault confrontation directly from the Roster-Assembly Table without guessing at what "feels right."
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:365: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each calibrate their difficulty against a single lair's escalation tier — how aggressively the party moved through **Faction Outposts**'s outposts, essentially. **Vault of Dragons** has no equivalent fresh escalation clock, because it doesn't need one: the Faction-State Summary Table already carries every difficulty variable the campaign has generated across its entire length. A party that dismantled Manshoon's Splinter in **Kolat Towers**, redeemed the Cassalanters in **Cassalanter Villa**, and allied with Jarlaxle in **Sea Maidens Faire** arrives at Scene 5 facing, essentially, nobody. A party that skipped **Kolat Towers**, left the Cassalanters Active-Adversarial, and made an enemy of Bregan D'aerthe arrives facing all three at once. This is not a punishment mechanic bolted onto the finale — it is the entire campaign's accumulated weight, made visible in a single afternoon, which is exactly what a convergence quest should do.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:373: The source material gestures at a contingency for a party that reaches this point with a damaged or incomplete Stone, but does not supply a mechanical framework for it beyond acknowledging the possibility exists. This document assumes the standard path: three Eyes recovered across **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, and **Sea Maidens Faire**, in any order. A table that has genuinely lost an Eye to destruction rather than simple non-recovery is in territory this document does not cover, and inventing an elaborate degraded-vault mechanic for a scenario the source material itself only gestures at would add more complexity than the edge case warrants. If it comes up, the simplest fix is usually the right one: let the party find another way to the same information through legwork, at a DC and a time cost the GM judges fair, rather than building a parallel ceremony for a version of the Stone that was never fully realized in the source design.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:219: The job was supposed to be clean. The Xanathar Guild was acquiring something from a rival organization; the character was hired to facilitate transit and paid well enough not to ask questions. What came out of there wasn't what they'd been told. What they left behind was considerably worse. The Guild got its item. The irregularities were absorbed into a larger accounting of that night, and the character's involvement was not officially noted.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:221: Ahmaergo — Xanathar's dwarven majordomo, the man who actually runs the organization's logistics — keeps a private ledger separate from the official records. The character's name is in it, under a column that reads *Outstanding.*
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:225: **The Holder: Ahmaergo (Xanathar's Guild)**
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:226: A compact, humorless dwarf who is in practical terms the operational brain of the Xanathar Guild. He doesn't have Xanathar's moods or its capacity for sudden violence, which makes him considerably more dangerous to deal with: he's consistent. His first formal request arrives as a logistics problem — something that requires someone unaffiliated, deniable, and competent. He won't call it debt repayment. Characters who complete the task will find the *Outstanding* column now reads *Current.* Characters who refuse will find the official records suddenly contain their name in a context that requires immediate explanation to the City Watch.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:228: > **Warning: The Beholder.** Ahmaergo maintains his private ledger precisely because Xanathar does not know about every arrangement. If Xanathar becomes aware of the character's existence — through carelessness, through another character's indiscretion, through any direct contact — the dynamic changes completely. Xanathar does not distinguish between asset, debtor, and enemy when its paranoia is engaged. Keep the beholder out of this relationship for as long as dramatically possible.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:260: Skeemo Weirdbottle maintains, out of a cramped and thoroughly unassuming shop in the Dock Ward, one of the most comprehensive indices of Waterdeep's criminal and semi-criminal activities that exists outside the Xanathar Guild's own records. He compiled it partly for business purposes and partly because he finds the city's power structures intrinsically interesting as a subject of study. He sold you something from it — access, a specific piece of information, a document that enabled something you needed to do — and conducted the transaction with professional discretion.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:7: > Villains: [Xanathar](../setting/villains/xanathar.md) · [Manshoon](../setting/villains/manshoon.md) · [The Cassalanters](../setting/villains/cassalanters.md) · [Jarlaxle Baenre](../setting/villains/jarlaxle.md)
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:39: - **Dock Ward** (port, crime, contraband):** the most dangerous ward; Xanathar Guild's primary surface territory; Sea Maidens Faire moors here during Fleetswake
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:91: *Era 2 — The Long Shadow (the Xanathar Guild's century)*
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:92: - The name "Xanathar" as a title that predates any individual beholder; the first holder established the criminal underworld's structure; subsequent Xanathars inherited the apparatus and the paranoia together
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:93: - The Guild's method of continuity: systematic elimination of anyone who knows more than Xanathar itself; what remains after a century of this is a surveillance network with no institutional memory beyond what the current beholder chooses to believe
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:94: - The current Xanathar's ascension: how it came to believe it is the original; why this belief is now structural to the organization's identity rather than correctable
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:95: - Skullport as the Guild's undercity foundation — the shadow city three levels below Waterdeep, outside city jurisdiction, processing everything the surface economy won't handle; the Xanathar Guild's relationship to Skullport is the relationship between a city and its sewers
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:133: - The convergence: all four factions move on Waterdeep in the same window — Manshoon's cell establishing Kolat Towers, Jarlaxle arriving with the Sea Maidens Faire, the Cassalanters accelerating their timeline, the Xanathar Guild responding to new pressure from competitors it hasn't yet fully identified
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:134: - Nar'l Xibrindas placed inside the Xanathar Guild by Jarlaxle before the campaign begins; intelligence about the Stone and the vault already flowing to Bregan D'aerthe

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:170: | Bregan D'aerthe | Full intelligence via Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar Guild | No Eye yet | Moving to acquire one; observing all other factions |
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:177: ### Xanathar
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:179: **Purpose:** Gives the DM the tools to portray Xanathar as a specific, three-dimensional villain: a paranoid intelligence that has built a criminal empire around its own terror of vulnerability, wearing the trappings of a monster because the trappings are convenient.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:199: | Ahmaergo | Majordomo | Most trusted lieutenant; the only one Xanathar registers as competent; Ahmaergo knows he is replaceable |

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:202: | Manshoon | Rival | Active enemy; Xanathar has ordered Manshoon killed twice; both failed; this is accelerating the paranoia |
